Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 211:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 5 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–14, stating the Fase C outcomes, which cover more than this Grade 5 book alone) plus one guide section per student chapter, with full answer keys (a 0–4 scoring rubric per test item), misconception notes, differentiation, remedial and enrichment tasks, parent interaction and reading lists (printed pp. 1–428). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| The area theorems: parallelograms on the same base and between the same parallels are equal in area, and triangles on the same base and of the same altitude are equal in area; a median divides a triangle into two triangles of equal area | Pakistan (Punjab), Class 9 (PCTB, eLearn.Punjab v1.1), Ch. 16, printed pp. 2–10 |
| The area of a triangle from its vertices, in determinant form; collinearity as the zero-area case | Pakistan (Punjab), Class 12 Mathematics (PCTB, eLearn.Punjab v1.1), Ch. 4, printed pp. 41–42 |
| Exploring perimeter by counting the unit lengths around a shape on a grid; exploring area — the area of a shape is the number of square units that cover its surface, found by counting squares | Syria, Grade 3 pupil's book, printed pp. 144–148 |
| Perimeter of a rectangle = the sum of its side lengths = 2 × (length + width), and perimeter of a square = 4 × side length, each by three methods — counting units, adding the sides, using the rule; area of a rectangle = length × width and area of a square = side × side, in cm² and m²; finding the side of a square from its area (a garden of 81 m² has side 9 m) | Syria, Grade 4 pupil's book, printed pp. 111–119 |
| The area of a triangle found by counting squares — a triangle is half of the rectangle on the same base and height; the altitude of a triangle: the perpendicular segment from a vertex to the opposite side (the base); area of a triangle = (base × height) ÷ 2; in a right triangle the hypotenuse and the two legs are named, and its area is half the product of the two legs | Syria, Grade 5 pupil's book, printed pp. 131–135 |
| π introduced: the ratio of a circle's circumference to its diameter is the same for every circle — the symbol π, the book's working value 3.14, and the historical values 3.16 (Egyptians) and 3.125 (Babylonians); the perimeter of a closed shape is the length of its boundary line (symbol P), all lengths in one unit; circumference of a circle = π × diameter = 2πr; perimeters of composite shapes; area (symbol A) as the surface a shape covers, measured by counting unit squares, found by cut-and-paste into a known shape and by splitting into familiar shapes; area of a right triangle = half the product of its two legs; area of a parallelogram = the length of a side × the height belonging to that side, obtained by cut-and-paste into a rectangle; a shape with the larger perimeter does not necessarily have the larger area, and two shapes can have equal areas with different perimeters | Syria, Grade 6 pupil's book, printed pp. 129–137, 156–163 |
| The height of a parallelogram relative to a base — any segment perpendicular to the two parallel lines carrying the base and the opposite side; area of a parallelogram = length of a side × the height belonging to that side, obtained by cutting and pasting the parallelogram into a rectangle of the same area; using the two ways of computing the same area to find a fourth length from three known ones (BC = 5 cm with height AH = 3 cm gives S = 15 cm²; with the second height AG = 3.75 cm, CD = 15/3.75 = 4 cm); area of a triangle = half the product of a base and its height, obtained by pasting two congruent copies of the triangle into a rectangle of twice its area; finding a triangle's height from its area and base (a tent door of base 3 m and area 3 m² has height 2 m); splitting a quadrilateral (or a plot of land) into two shapes whose areas can be computed | Syria, Grade 7 student's book, printed pp. 56–58, 143–145 |
| The height of an isosceles trapezium found by Pythagoras on the congruent end triangles, then its area as half the sum of the bases times the height; the lateral surface area of a cone is the area of its net's circular sector, the sector's area being proportional to its central angle (360° gives the whole disc of radius equal to the generator) — worked for radius 3 and generator 5 in the deepening exercises | Syria, Grade 8 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 82–83, 115 |
| Lateral surface area of a right prism or a right cylinder equals the base perimeter × the height; total surface area adds twice the base area (worked cylinder, r = 7.5 cm and h = 40 cm: lateral area S_L = 2π × 7.5 × 40 = 600π cm²; total area S_T = 600π + 2π × 7.5² = 712.5π cm²) | Syria, Grade 9 Geometry student's book, printed p. 63 |
| The integral as area (Theorem 9): for f continuous and ≥ 0 on [a, b], ∫ₐᵇ f is the area enclosed by the graph, the x-axis and the lines x = a, x = b — the proof shows the area function x ↦ A(x) is itself an antiderivative of f via a rectangle squeeze; signed versus true area: for f ≤ 0 the area is −∫ₐᵇ f (Result 10), and for a sign-changing f the interval is split at the zeros and the part-areas added — the true area is ∫ₐᵇ |f| (Result 11), while ∫ₐᵇ f alone is only the algebraic (signed) sum (worked in full for f(x) = 4x − 4x² on [0, 1], area 2/3); the area between two curves is ∫ₐᵇ |f − g| | Syria, Grade 12 Scientific, Part One, printed pp. 218–222 |

| Perimeter as the distance around a closed figure, with mixed units converted first; fencing applications — items at intervals and poles around gardens, with cost; area by counting unit squares, area units km²–mm² and land units (hectare, are, centiare, acre); area of a rectangle A = l × w | Rwanda, Primary Learner's Mathematics P4, pupil's book, Unit 6 (§§6.7–6.8), printed pp. 83–88; Unit 9 (§§9.1–9.4), printed pp. 107–113 |

| Trapezium area — Trapezium شبه المنحرف defined (only two parallel sides); bases, legs, height; consecutive angles on a leg supplementary; midline = half the sum of the bases; area derived via the midline cut → ½ × (sum of bases) × height | Sudan, Grade 6 Mathematics, printed p. 148 |
| Arc length — Arc length = (central angle / 360) × circumference (4-2) | Sudan, Grade 9 Mathematics (Third Year Intermediate), printed p. 128 |

| Perimeter and area problems — Tiling a kitchen floor; a square carrom board of perimeter 320 cm — find its area; card table (length, width, perimeter, area — find the missing one) | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 148 |
| Belt problem — A belt made from postcard strips: its length/perimeter is not fixed by its area; how to get a longer belt from the same postcard | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 150 |
| Big area units — One square metre (how many children fit in it); one square kilometre and people per square km | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 151 |

| Doubling a square — Square B with double the side of square A (9 sq cm): perimeter doubles, area becomes four times (36 sq cm) | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 154 |

| Perimeter as the distance around a closed figure; perimeter by adding all sides | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 205 |
| Perimeter formulas — Perimeter of a rectangle = 2 × (length + breadth); square = 4 × side; equilateral triangle = 3 × side; regular polygon = sides × side | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 208 |
| Area estimating — Area by counting squares on squared paper, with the half-square convention (less than half ignored, more than half counted as 1) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 214 |
| Area formulas — Area of a rectangle = length × breadth; area of a square = side × side; splitting shapes into rectangles | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 217 |
| Review — Perimeter and area of squares and rectangles recalled (perimeter of a rectangle = 2(l + b), area = l × b), incl. perimeter change when a square is cut off a sheet | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 205 |
| Triangle area — A triangle cut along a rectangle's diagonal has half the rectangle's area; area of a triangle = ½ × base × height | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 209 |
| Parallelogram area — Area of a parallelogram = base × corresponding height, by cutting and rearranging into a rectangle; same-perimeter parallelograms can have different areas | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 210 |
| Area units — Converting area units (cm² ↔ m² ↔ hectares) and path/cross-road area applications | India, Mathematics Class 7, printed p. 225 |
| Area recalled — perimeters/areas of rectangle, square, parallelogram, triangle and circle recalled | India, Mathematics Class 8, printed p. 169 |
| Trapezium — area of a trapezium = ½ × (sum of parallel sides) × distance between them, derived by splitting/doubling | India, Mathematics Class 8, printed p. 171 |
| General quadrilateral — area of a general quadrilateral by triangulation using a diagonal and two perpendiculars | India, Mathematics Class 8, printed p. 173 |

| Polygons — area of a polygon by dividing into triangles, trapeziums and rectangles | India, Mathematics Class 8, printed p. 175 |
| Parallelogram and triangle area — area of parallelogram = base × altitude; triangle on same base/parallels is half the parallelogram (proved as example/corollary) | India, Mathematics Class 9, printed p. 158 |
| Heron's formula stated (NOT proved): area = √[s(s−a)(s−b)(s−c)], s = (a+b+c)/2; checked against ½ × base × height on a right triangle | India, Mathematics Class 9, printed p. 199 |
| Heron's formula applied — areas of triangles from three sides (worked park/plot examples, incl. ratio-of-sides problems) | India, Mathematics Class 9, printed p. 200 |
| Heron for quadrilaterals — area of a quadrilateral by splitting on a diagonal and applying Heron twice; rhombus field split by a diagonal | India, Mathematics Class 9, printed p. 203 |
| Circle measures recalled — circumference 2πr and area πr² recalled; π = circumference/diameter | India, Mathematics Class 10, printed p. 224 |
| Sectors — sector (minor/major) and segment defined; arc length = (θ/360) × 2πr; area of a sector = (θ/360) × πr² | India, Mathematics Class 10, printed p. 226 |
| Segments — area of a segment = area of sector − area of the corresponding triangle (worked: segment AYB) | India, Mathematics Class 10, printed p. 228 |
| Combined figures — areas of combinations of plane figures (flower beds, shaded designs, racetrack-type regions) | India, Mathematics Class 10, printed p. 231 |
| Perimeter introduced — finding a shape's surrounding length (Хүрээний урт): Bat's father will decorate his fence with a light garland — the L-shaped fence has sides 5 m, 11 m, 6 m, 20 m, 5 m, 25 m; how many metres of garland does he need?; measure the sides of shapes and find the sum of all their side lengths; the definition: the sum of the lengths of all the sides of a shape is called its perimeter (периметр); an activity: draw any shape, measure each side with a ruler, find its perimeter, compare shapes and perimeters with friends; Boldoo has 15 cm of wire and wants to decorate a shape's edge — which shapes can he decorate (triangle 4 cm, 4 cm, 7 cm; rectangle 3 cm by 5 cm; pentagon 3 cm sides; rhombus 4 cm sides)? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 58 |
| Perimeter problems — find the perimeters of the equal-sided shapes — what is the easy way? (triangle, rhombus, hexagon, pentagon pictured); a quadrilateral has all sides 5 cm: if each side is doubled, how does its perimeter change?; calculate the pictured shapes' perimeters by counting grid cells, without a ruler (1 cell = 1 cm); find your desk top's perimeter using your span — how many spans is it?; a 15 cm wire is to be divided into 3 equal parts — how many cuts?; complete the quadrilateral table (perimeter and three sides given, find the fourth: 100 dm with 28, 32, 18; □ with 25 cm, 28 cm, 31 cm, 15 cm; 99 cm with 23 m?? (units as printed) 20 m, □, 28 m; 64 cm with 16 cm, □, 16 cm, 16 cm; 80 cm with □, 18 cm, 20 cm, 26 cm); father is fencing a square sandpit of 5 m sides with 2 m planks — how many planks go in?; uncle fenced a rectangular vegetable field of length 10 m and width 3 m less than the length — how many metres of fence did he build?; the boy repaired 59 m of road on the first day and 40 m less on the second — how many metres in all?; measure your classroom's board and walls with your arm-span and the door's length with your span, and record them | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 59 |
| Area as counting unit squares — measuring area (Талбайг хэмжих): the kindergarten's workers decided to cover part of the grounds with square-shaped slabs and to build a fence around the plot: how many slabs will be needed — how do you know? What length of fence is needed to go around that plot of land?; the children built various shapes with yellow squares (six shapes A-E built from unit squares on grids): from how many squares does each shape consist? Complete the number table (shape A: 6 squares, given); the definition box: "The number of equal unit squares that fit inside a closed flat shape is that shape's area measure (талбайн хэмжээ). For example, shape A's area measure is 6 squares"; draw a composition built from same-size square shapes and find the composition's area measure; let's find a rectangle's area measure: a. draw the shapes shown in the picture with the given counts and cut them out ready (a big rectangle outline labelled 1 piece; a pink square labelled 4 pieces; a small yellow square labelled 8 pieces); b. from how many big squares does the rectangle consist?; c. from how many small squares does the rectangle consist?; d. which of the numbers found is the rectangle's area measure? Why? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 73 |
| The square centimetre; area versus perimeter — two children's speeches: the boy: "Depending on the size of the one square used for measuring, a shape's area measure can come out differently. So the area measurement's unit must be written after the number"; the girl: "People use definite units when measuring area"; the unit box: area can be measured with a square of side 1 cm: such a unit is marked "1 cm.kv" (1 см.кв) and read "1 square centimetre" (1 сантиметр квадрат); worked example 1: find the shape's area measure (the stepped shape consists of 5 squares of side 1 cm, so its area measure is 5 cm.kv); find the shape's boundary length (perimeter): the shape has 6 sides: find the sum of these sides' lengths: 2 cm + 2 cm + 1 cm + 1 cm + 1 cm + 3 cm = 10 cm; find the shapes' area measures and perimeters (three stepped shapes with 1 cm side marks); two pupils put forward their ideas for finding a rectangle's perimeter and area measure: complete their calculations and discuss the methods: Pupil 1: find the perimeter: □ cm + □ cm + □ cm + □ cm = □ cm; counting, the rectangle consists of □ squares of 1 cm square, so the area measure is □ cm.kv; Pupil 2: the rectangle's length is □ cm and its width is □ cm; the opposite sides are of equal length; find the perimeter: 2 × □ cm + 2 × □ cm = 2 × (□ + □) cm = □ cm; the rectangle consists of □ × □ = □ squares, so the area measure is □ cm.kv | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 74 |
| Area from rounded measures; same area, different perimeter — measure the shapes' side lengths and round them to centimetres: using the rounded measurements, find the shapes' area measures and explain (five shapes: a stepped polyomino; a plain rectangle; a square with a square hole; a stepped shape; a U-shape; below, a boy and a girl show a rectangle being cut and rearranged — cutting off a corner piece and moving it — to find an area); the school's playground is square-shaped: its side is 100 m long: Bat walked once around the playground: if he walks around it twice, how much distance will he travel (100 × 4 × 2 = 800 m)?; a room and the things that can be placed in it are shown with shapes (a floor-plan grid: the room, a clothes wardrobe, a bed, a play corner, a desk, a bookshelf, a clock and a chair drawn as rectangles with their sizes): a. draw the big rectangle "room" in your notebook; b. taking a square as having a side of 1 cm, draw on paper the things pictured on the room's right side with their measurements; c. choose the table, the wardrobe and so on from the picture and draw them placed in the "room"; d. on what area of the floor do the drawn things lie in the "room"? Find the floor's unoccupied area measure; draw shapes with the given area measures and compare their perimeters (6 cm.kv; 12 cm.kv; 9 cm.kv; 18 cm.kv; 5 cm.kv); find the pattern: from how many squares do the 4th and the 9th shapes consist (a sequence of growing zigzag shapes built from squares)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 75 |
| Area test and consolidation — a test (Сорих): write the area measures of shapes A, B and C (three polyominoes on squared grids); for shape D (Г — a U-shaped outline), measure its sides, round to centimetres, and find its perimeter and its area measure; a rectangle has length 6 cm and width 4 cm: find that rectangle's perimeter and area measure (20 cm and 24 cm.kv); consolidation (Бататгах): the children made compositions from squares of side 1 cm, their halves and equal triangles (four tangram-like compositions): a. find the area measures of the compositions the children built; b. cut out squares and triangles, make compositions yourself and find their areas; from the given area measure, draw different shapes: can other shapes be drawn? From the shapes with the same area measure, find the shape with the biggest perimeter (a. 10 squares; b. 8 squares; c. 16 squares; d. 24 squares; one square is 1 cell — an H-shaped figure is pictured); measure the given shape's side lengths (an H-like stepped shape): find its perimeter; a rectangle is 6 cm long and 2 cm wide: if its length is decreased 2 times, how will the rectangle's perimeter and area measure change?; draw rectangles whose perimeter is 10 cm: find their area measures; three papers' total area measure is 198 cm.kv: the first and second papers' area measures sum to 108 cm.kv, and the second and third papers' sum to 145 cm.kv: find each paper's area measure (first 53, second 55, third 90 cm.kv) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 76 |
| Area by counting unit squares; rectangle area formula — measuring area (Талбайг хэмжих): find the rectangle's area measure (to find a shape's area measure, calculate how many unit square shapes fit wholly inside the shape: a square whose sides are 1 cm is written 1 cm.kv; the pictured rectangle holds the labelled 1 cm.kv squares); a girl's note: "In this rectangle, the 1 cm.kv square shape fitted wholly 6 times, so the rectangle's area measure is 6 cm.kv"; draw a square with sides of 10 cm in your notebook: guess how many squares of side 1 cm fit into it (100): what unit is the area measure of this 10 cm square called (1 dm.kv)?; find the shapes' area measures (three shapes on centimetre grids: a rectangle, a big square and an H-shaped polygon); observe how the perimeter and the area measure of a rectangle with sides 9 cm and 4 cm are found, and discuss (the boxes: (9 cm + 4 cm) × 2 = 26 cm — the perimeter is the sum of all the rectangle's sides; 4 cm × 9 cm = 36 cm.kv — the area measure is the length measure multiplied by the width measure); a rectangle-shaped paper is 20 cm long and 10 cm wide: find its area measure and its perimeter; a rectangle's area is 100 cm.kv and its width is 5 cm: find its length and its perimeter; a rectangle's area is 300 cm.kv and its length is 60 cm: find its width and its perimeter; a self-check: draw rectangles with an area measure of 8 cm.kv in your notebook: how many such rectangles can there be? | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 18 |
| Area units: cm², dm², m², km², are, hectare — name and mark the area measurement units as follows (a box pictures a 1 m square grid with one black 1 cm.kv corner square and lists the units: square centimetre (см.кв), square decimetre (дм.кв), square metre (м.кв), square kilometre (км.кв), are (а) and hectare (га)); a boy's working: 1 m = 10 dm; 1 m × 1 m = 1 m.kv; 10 dm × 10 dm = 100 dm.kv = 1 m.kv; the unit relations: 1 cm.kv = 100 mm.kv; 1 dm.kv = 100 cm.kv; 1 m.kv = 100 dm.kv; 1 are = 100 m.kv; 1 hectare = 10000 m.kv; express the area measure using the length measurement unit (a. 1 dm.kv = □ cm × □ cm = □ cm.kv; b. 1 m.kv = □ cm × □ cm = □ cm.kv; c. 1 m.kv = □ dm × □ dm = □ dm.kv; d. 1 km.kv = □ m × □ m = □ m.kv); express the area measure in the indicated unit (5 dm.kv = □ cm.kv; 7 m.kv = □ cm.kv; 3 m.kv = □ dm.kv; 4 km.kv = □ m.kv; 5 km.kv = □ dm.kv; 2 km.kv = □ hectares); compare (1 m.kv □ 25 dm.kv; 2 hectares □ 10000 m.kv; 4 dm.kv □ 60 cm.kv; 2 km.kv □ 20 hectares; 900 cm.kv □ 30 m.kv; 1 m.kv □ 1000 cm.kv); find the rectangles' area measures and perimeters (a. a rectangle 6 m by 2 m; b. a square 8 cm by 8 cm; c. a rectangle 10 cm by 2 cm); an activity: 1. divide the pictured grid shape into three shapes equal in shape and measure using a ruler: find the divided shapes' area measures and perimeters; 2. a rectangle's perimeter is 16 m: what can its length and width measures be? Express the lengths and widths in whole numbers; a self-check: 1) express the area measure in the indicated unit (4 km.kv = □ m.kv; 20 m.kv = □ dm.kv; 16 dm.kv = □ cm.kv); 2) a rectangle's area measure is 500 cm.kv and its width is 25 cm: find its length and its perimeter | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 19 |
| Completing shapes to rectangles; symmetry and area — complete the following shapes up to a rectangle shape and find their area measures: count each cell as 1 cm.kv (seven numbered shapes on grids: a square, a long rectangle, a parallelogram, a right-angled triangle, two trapezoids and an isosceles triangle — each is completed to a rectangle, and the triangle and parallelogram areas come out as the whole or half of it); a rectangle is 8 cm long and 5 cm wide: find its area and its perimeter; draw a rectangle whose area is 18 cm.kv; a rectangle's area is 12 cm.kv and its perimeter is 16 cm: find its sides (6 cm and 2 cm); calculate the shapes' area measures and build the shapes symmetric with respect to the line (three half-shapes are drawn beside vertical axis lines on grids: a half-trapezoid, a slanted half-parallelogram and a half-hexagon — complete each shape by symmetry, then find its area); express the area measure in the indicated unit (3 km.kv = □ m.kv; 2500 dm.kv = □ m.kv; 5 m.kv = □ dm.kv; 10000 cm.kv = □ m.kv; 25 dm.kv = □ cm.kv; 8 m.kv = □ cm.kv); find the area measure of the uncoloured part (three grid pictures: a rectangle outline holding a blue inner rectangle; a square holding a yellow diamond; a big rectangle holding a green triangle) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 21 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 72 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, North Macedonia, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Tanzania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Peru, Suriname, Papua New Guinea, Timor-Leste, Uganda, Samoa).** Sierra Leone carries the cleanest Iran counts squares for area in Grade 3, derives the parallelogram and triangle areas in Grade 4, adds the rhombus and trapezium in Grade 5, and reaches polygon areas in Grade 10 Geometry (1) and Heron's formula in Grade 11 Geometry (2).
ladder — perimeter short cuts, rectangle and triangle areas with
the perpendicular-height rule, and the parallelogram, trapezium
and composite areas in JSS 2. Jordan carries perimeter and area
from Grade 3 through the parallelogram, triangle and trapezium
areas in Grade 6. Egypt carries the whole investigation — same
area with different perimeters and same perimeter with different
areas — from Grade 3 (with perimeter and area explored as counts
in Primary 3 Term 2), and the parallelogram, triangle and
trapezium areas in Grade 6 (rows completed by the 2026-11-08
re-verification sweep). Greece carries the parallelogram
area by slanting and the triangle as half the parallelogram in
Grade 6. Kazakhstan carries the perimeter formulas in Grade 2
and the rectangle, square and right-triangle areas in Grade 3.
Libya carries area and perimeter from Grade 3 through compound
shapes in Grade 4. Mauritania carries perimeters of squares,
rectangles, parallelograms, rhombuses and trapezoids in Grades 4
and 5. Vietnam carries triangle, trapezium and circle areas in
Grade 5. South Africa carries perimeter and area across Grades 5,
7 and 8. Armenia carries rectangle and square area in Grade 5.
Ghana's evidence is teacher-manual only (JICA B5 Lessons 92–100):
no pupil textbook in Ghana's set teaches perimeter and area, and
the lesson words Ghana that way throughout. Laos carries perimeter and area from rectangle and square area in Grade 4, through the full set — rectangle, square, triangle, parallelogram, trapezoid and circle — in Grade 5, to dissection proofs in Grade 7, with the Grade 5 circle-area slip corrected to 78.5 cm². Yemen carries perimeter and area from perimeter and area in Grade 3, through rectangles and squares, to parallelograms, triangles, rhombuses, trapeziums and circles. Indonesia carries perimeter and area from area by counting unit squares in Grade 4 — with no "length × width" formula stated and no perimeter taught at all — to perimeter as the sum of side lengths and the full set of shape areas derived by cutting and rearranging in Grade 5. Nicaragua carries perimeter and area from triangle perimeter in Grade 3, through rectangle and square area with the Nicaraguan land units vara cuadrada and manzana in Grade 4, to polygon perimeters and parallelogram/triangle area in Grade 5, trapezium, rhombus, circle and composite area in Grade 6, and the full polygon/circle/sector range in Grade 7 (student book and module) and the Grade 8 module, with equilateral-triangle and hexagon areas by Pythagoras in Grade 9. Ethiopia carries perimeter and area from rectangle perimeter and area in Grade 4, through area by counting unit squares and rectangle area in Grade 5 (no perimeter formula work there — the guide expects square and rectangle perimeter in §8.4, guide-only), to perimeter and area of triangles, quadrilaterals, circles and composite shapes in Grade 7, perimeter and area of similar triangles in Grade 8, the area of a regular polygon in Grade 9, and segment and sector perimeter and area in Grade 10. South Sudan carries perimeter and area from rectangle and square area in Grade 4 (no perimeter taught there), through acres, hectares and triangle area in Grade 6 and quadrilateral areas in Grade 7, to the full perimeter and area range in Grade 8, perimeter, circumference and area in Secondary 1, and area estimation by grid counting and the mid-ordinate and trapezium rules in Secondary 4 — with the Primary 4 room worked as 2 × 3 = 6 m² corrected to 4 × 6 = 24 m², the Primary 6 area example's unexplained r = 14 set aside for the r = 7 cm → 154 cm² example (p. 25), and the Primary 7 trapezium property "No sides, angles and diagonals are equal" corrected (an isosceles trapezium has equal legs, equal base angles and equal diagonals). Iraq carries perimeter and area from measuring area by counting unit squares in Grade 3, through square and rectangle perimeter and area in Grade 4 and triangle area in Grade 5, to parallelogram, trapezium, circle and composite-shape area in Grade 6, scale effect on perimeter and area and paving area in Grade 7, composite-shape area in Grade 8, and regular-polygon perimeter and area in Grade 9. Bangladesh carries perimeter and area from rectangle and square perimeter and area in Grade 4, through rectangle, parallelogram and triangle areas in Grade 5, measuring shapes by counting grid squares in Grade 6, the perimeter/area table in Grade 7, quadrilateral areas and sector and segment area in Grade 8, to the surface areas of cones, spheres, prisms and pyramids in Grade 9. Nepal carries perimeter and area from area by counting unit squares in Grade 3, through the perimeter of a rectangle only with area by counting squares in Grade 4 — no area or perimeter formulas for a square or triangle there — to rectangle and square perimeter and area formulas in Grade 5, perimeter, area and volume in Grade 6, square and rectangle perimeter in Grade 7, the full set of shape areas in Grade 8, Heron's formula with Nepali land units (1 ropani = 16 aana, 1 aana = 31.79 sq. m) in Grade 9, and triangle and quadrilateral area in Grade 10. Cambodia carries perimeter and area whole from polygon perimeters and shape areas in Grade 6 through triangle, parallelogram, trapezoid and rhombus areas in Grades 7–8, with triangle area by sine and Heron's formula in Grade 10. Kuwait carries perimeter and area from Grade 3 lessons 4-9 and 4-10 and Grade 4 rectangle-and-square area, through perimeter and area of basic shapes in Grade 6, to area work in the Grade 6, 7 and 8 measurement units. Band C adds the trigonometric area of a triangle (½ab sin C) and Heron's formula in Grade 11 Term 2 (scientific track). North Macedonia enters from its main set — Grade 4 (2023), Grade 5 (2022), Grade 6 (2023), Grade 7 (2025) and Grade 11 (2026).

China carries perimeter and area from Grade 3 rectangle and square area, through Grade 4 cut-and-shift composites, to arc and sector work in Grade 9 and the ½ab sin C and Heron formulas in the senior books.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries perimeter and area from Grade 3's first formulas through Class 6's derived parallelogram, trapezium and triangle areas to Class 9's equal-area theorems.

Syria explores perimeter and area by counting in Grade 3, gives the rectangle and square rules in Grade 4, derives the triangle's area as half a rectangle in Grade 5, introduces π with its Egyptian and Babylonian values in Grade 6, derives the parallelogram's and triangle's areas by cutting and pasting in Grades 6–7, and closes the arc in Grade 12, where the integral itself is defined as area and signed area is separated from true area.

Saudi Arabia separates the two measures cleanly: area by covering and counting squares from Grade 2, perimeter as the sum of the sides in Grade 3, then both formulas in Grade 4 with estimation first and the unknown side found from the area — witnessed by the older 1445 Term-3 volumes, with practice evidence from the partial exercises book.

Malaysia derives its plane area formulae in Form 1 by cutting and rearranging — never just stating them — and returns to area at the top end with the shoelace formula for coordinates and ½ab sin C with Heron's formula for triangles.

Rwanda teaches perimeter and area from Year 2's side-by-side measuring and first formulas, through Year 3's formulas worked both ways, Year 4's fencing problems, land units and derived areas for triangle, parallelogram, rhombus and trapezium, to Year 6's regular-polygon perimeter and area.

Sudan teaches perimeter in Grade 5 (triangle, square, rectangle, including recovering a side from the perimeter) and area in Grade 6 (rectangle, square, parallelogram, triangle, rhombus, trapezium), returning for arc length in Grade 9.

India carries perimeter and area through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 4–5 and Mathematics Class 6–10 — including perimeter as boundary length; same area, different perimeter; perimeter as the distance around a closed figure; perimeter by adding all sides; review.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X, XII (Grades 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12): perimeter introduced; perimeter problems; area as counting unit squares.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Triángulos, rectángulos y algo más...* (Grade 2), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7) and *Naturaleza Matemática* (Grade 10). From its books: from Grade 2: perimeter — the sum of the measures of a figure's sides is its perímetro (tangram pieces measured and summed); from Grade 6: grid areas — Areas inside a tessellation grid (9 cm × 6 cm rectangle, coloured squares and triangles) using A = b × h and A = b × h / 2; from Grade 10: total length of a composite track (straights + two semicircle arcs) ≈ 3.56 km.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 4: perimeter of a triangle (Lesson 23); perimeter of a rectangle (Lesson 24); perimeter of a square (Lesson 25); perimeter of a pentagon (Lesson 26); perimeter of a hexagon (Lesson 27); surface (area) — first introduction (Lesson 28); the unit of area; area of a square (Lesson 30); area of a rectangle (Lesson 31); from Grade 6: units of area and their conversions; area of a rectangle; area of a square; area of a triangle; area of a rhombus; area of a parallelogram, including by a diagonal and its altitude; area of a trapezium; area of an irregular quadrilateral by splitting it into triangles; from Grade 10: area of a circular sector; area of a circular segment; area of a triangle from two sides and the included angle; area of a triangle from three sides (Heron's formula).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): Perimeter of regular figures; determining perimeter; Area (syprina) by counting/unit squares and formula work. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): Opener + 10A: area and perimeter of rectilinear figures (plans 93–95); : area and perimeter of compound figures (plans 98–99). from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .1 Area of a triangle (plan 10); .2 Area of a parallelogram and of a trapezium (plan 11). from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Perimeter and area of plane figures incl. the trapezium. from Grade 12 (teacher's book): Syprina e trekëndëshave (Area of a triangle with a suitable formula, ½ab sin C form).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Problemas con ángulos y segmentos; Polígonos regulares e irregulares; Perímetro de polígonos; Perímetros aplicados; Área de figuras planas; Áreas trabajadas; Problemas aplicados de áreas y perímetros; from Grade 8: Valor numérico en geometría; Perímetros con polinomios; Áreas con polinomios; from Grade 12: Problema de áreas (axiomas); from the Compendium's Secundaria 1 section (Grade 7): Perímetros, áreas y formas geométricas.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Area as counting unit squares; from Grade 11: History of geometry, part 3: Heron's Metrica (1st century BC), where Heron's formula for…

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires measuring the perimeter of objects and shapes using standard units; Class 2 adds determining the area of regular and irregular shapes by counting squares and the area of the square, rectangle and triangle; Classes 3 and 4 require the perimeter of regular and irregular shapes and the area of regular and irregular shapes with their units — Class 4 adding the length of a rectangle found from its area or perimeter and the other dimension. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: perimeter of rectangle, square and triangle revised; area as space covered, counted in equal square units; the areas of rectangle, square and triangle by formula, each also worked backwards to a missing side, base or height.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Geometry 8: the concept of a polygon's area; area of a square; area of a rectangle; area of a parallelogram; area of a triangle; heron's formula; area of a rhombus; area of a trapezoid.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 28 teacher-evidence items from 13 of the guides (Book 2, Book 5, Book 10, Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 26, Book 27, Book 28, Book 29, Book 30; Grades 2, 3, 4, 5, 6).

## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 5 items (3 from student books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S5, Book S6, Book T5, Book S7, Book T6.

**Book S5:** *Libro de texto, 3.° grado* (student textbook, Grade 3, MINED 2026-tree edition) — El Salvador, MINEDUCYT (ESMATE programme, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019); 14,754,711 bytes, 196 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's own printed folios.

Cited by unit. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide reproduces the student book's lessons with lesson-by-lesson teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test (*Prueba de la unidad*) per unit; it is cited as teacher evidence, never as a pupil textbook.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 45 items (18 from pupil books, 27 teacher evidence) from Book S2, Book S3, Book S4, Book S7, Book S10, Book S12, Book S13, Book S14, Book S15, Book S16, Book S17, Book S26, Book T1, Book T2, Book T3, Book T4, Book T5, Book T6, Book T8, Book T9, Book T10, Book T11, Book T12, Book T13, Book T14, Book T15, Book T16, Book T17, Book T18, Book T19, Book T39, Book T40.

## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 8 items from Book 4, Book 7, Book 8, Book 11, Book 12, Book 17, Book 19.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 4 teacher-evidence items from Book 2.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Andorra's Segona ensenyança programme requires: (1r) perimeter and area: relations and differences | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (Decret 355/2023) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 36 |
| Andorra's Segona ensenyança programme requires: (1r) direct and indirect measure of surfaces | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (Decret 355/2023) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 36 |
| Andorra's Segona ensenyança programme requires: (1r) the relation between the area of the rectangle and the product of two lengths | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (Decret 355/2023) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 37 |
| Andorra's Segona ensenyança programme requires: (1r) calculating perimeters and areas of plane figures, varying one magnitude and fixing the other, finding maximum and minimum values in simple cases | Andorra — *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (Decret 355/2023) (curriculum programme) — teacher evidence (curriculum requirement), printed p. 37 |

Andorra enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Andorra's in-hand set is an official curriculum programme, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Andorra's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 programmes are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand — none exists to hold for the Escola Andorrana — and the Spanish and French systems' maths manuals are list-only names with no files held.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 8 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 3, Book 4, Book 5 and Book 6.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

## Moldova added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Moldova's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **12 books, all pupil textbooks: the Matematică / Mathematics student manual for each of Grades 1–12 (Republic of Moldova, in Romanian). There is no teacher guide and no syllabus in the Moldova Maths set in hand, so every Moldova row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Moldova counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 394 teaching items. The expansion map's “12 student + 0 teacher” is correct: textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files reconcile exactly at 12 distinct works — Grades 3–12 (10 books) recorded in books.json, Grades 1–2 as git-blob files recorded in textbooks.tsv. The old closed-52 inventory's “Moldova link-only, 0 in hand” is refuted: all 12 books sit secured in the tensae-code/liqlearns-textbooks GitHub repo and were read from there for this expansion (Grades 3, 4 and 6–12 via the mid-size-books-8 release; Grades 1, 2 and 5 via the Contents API on their textbooks.tsv git-blob paths), byte-verified — MD5s match books.json exactly for the ten Grades 3–12 books. The Grades 10–12 books are dual-track (profil real / profil umanistic) throughout; each section is stocked once, as one Moldova row, not once per track. Book errors: none found that changes a placement; the Grade 9 file's PDF p. 178 carries a stray page from another book (a biology text page) bound after the back cover in the publisher's print file — a file artifact, not part of the mathematics manual, cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the “Cine seamănă culege” review blocks, the Recapitulare / sistematizare / evaluare sections, the “Să recapitulăm” summaries, the recap exercise sets and “Test sumativ” pages closing chapters, and the “Răspunsuri (și indicații)” answer keys — are stocked nowhere, except where a review section is a topic's only statement, which is then marked as such. Printed-page bases: Book 4 — printed page = PDF page; Book 5 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Book 9 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Book 10 — printed pages as listed in the book's own Cuprins. Into this lesson the placements stock 9 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 4, Book 5, Book 9 and Book 10.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 15 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). ## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 3 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 1 and Book 2.

Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–52. Printed page = PDF page. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.
